## 3. CONTENTS OF KEY FILES

### requirements.txt (ROS2 top level)
**File:** `/home/jinwz/FoundationPoseROS2/FoundationPoseROS2/requirements.txt` (55 lines)

Key packages: `torchvision==0.16.0`, `torch==2.1.0`, `torchaudio==2.1.0`, `numpy<2`, `opencv_python==4.10.0.82`, `ultralytics==8.3.31`, `trimesh==4.4.7`, `pytorch_lightning==2.4.0`, `warp==1.0.4`, `scipy==1.14.1`, `pyrender==0.1.45`, `open3d==0.18.0`, `cv_bridge`, `kornia==0.5.10`, `PySide6==6.8.0.1`, `dearpygui==1.11.1`, etc.

### README.md
**File:** `/home/jinwz/FoundationPoseROS2/FoundationPoseROS2/README.md` (175 lines)

- **Title:** FoundationPoseROS2: Multi-Object Pose Estimation and Tracking of Novel Objects in ROS2 with RealSense2
- **Author:** ammar-n-abbas
- **Description:** ROS2-integrated system using FoundationPose + SAM2 for multi-object 6D pose estimation/tracking
- **Prerequisites:** Ubuntu 20/22, ROS2 Foxy/Humble, 8GB NVIDIA GPU, CUDA 12.x, Intel RealSense Camera
- **Setup:** Conda env `foundationpose_ros` with Python 3.10, run `build_all_conda.sh`
- **Important note:** Must change C++14 to C++17 in `FoundationPose/bundlesdf/mycuda/setup.py` lines 18-19
- **Running:** Launch realsense2_camera, then `python foundationpose_ros_multi.py`
- **Features:** Object Selection GUI (Tkinter), SAM2 segmentation, multi-object tracking, 6D pose publishing via PoseStamped

### run_foundationpose.sh
**File:** `/home/jinwz/FoundationPoseROS2/FoundationPoseROS2/run_foundationpose.sh` (27 lines)

Wrapper script that:
1. Activates conda env `foundationpose_ros`
2. Sets `CUDA_HOME` and `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`
3. Sources ROS2 Humble (or Foxy as fallback)
4. Runs `foundationpose_ros_multi.py`

### Dockerfile
**File:** `/home/jinwz/FoundationPoseROS2/FoundationPoseROS2/Dockerfile` (32 lines)

- Based on `ros:humble`
- Installs `ros-humble-cv-bridge`, `ros-humble-sensor-msgs`, `ros-humble-geometry-msgs`
- Pip installs: `numpy`, `scipy`, `trimesh`, `ultralytics`, `pyquaternion`, `pillow`, `rospkg`, `rclpy`
- Entrypoint: `source /opt/ros/humble/setup.bash && python3 main.py`

### foundationpose_ros_multi.py
**File:** `/home/jinwz/FoundationPoseROS2/FoundationPoseROS2/foundationpose_ros_multi.py` (394 lines)

This is THE main ROS2 node. Key structure:

- **Monkey-patches** FoundationPose to add `is_register` flag on `__init__` and `register`
- **FileSelectorGUI** (Tkinter): GUI to reorder .obj/.stl mesh files
- **PoseEstimationNode** (rclpy Node):
  - Subscribes to:
    - `/camera/camera/color/image_raw` (RGB)
    - `/camera/camera/aligned_depth_to_color/image_raw` (Depth)
    - `/camera/camera/color/camera_info` (Camera intrinsics)
  - Uses SAM2 (`ultralytics.SAM("sam2.1_b.pt")`) for segmentation
  - Interactive mask selection via OpenCV mouse callbacks
  - Creates one `FoundationPose` instance per object
  - Publishes `PoseStamped` on topics `/Current_OBJ_position_{idx+1}`
  - Transforms poses from camera frame to base frame via `cam_2_base_transform.py`
- Entry point: scans `demo_data/` for .obj/.stl files, opens GUI, then spins

### build_all_conda.sh
**File:** `/home/jinwz/FoundationPoseROS2/FoundationPoseROS2/build_all_conda.sh` (47 lines)

Steps:
1. Install PyTorch 2.1.0+cu121, torchvision, torchaudio
2. Install pytorch3d from source
3. Install `requirements.txt`
4. Clone FoundationPose repo
5. Download pretrained weights via gdown
6. Build pybind11 (v2.10.0)
7. Install Eigen 3.4.0
8. Clone and install nvdiffrast
9. Build mycpp C++ extensions
10. Build bundlesdf/mycuda CUDA extensions

## 6. ROS2 WORKSPACE STRUCTURE ANALYSIS

**This is NOT a colcon workspace.** Evidence:

1. **No** `package.xml` at any level (zero found)
2. **No** `setup.py` or `setup.cfg` at the top level (only inside FoundationPose submodules)
3. **No** standard ROS2 package directory layout (`src/`, `launch/`, `config/`, etc.)
4. The VS Code settings reference `/opt/ros/humble` but only for extra import paths, not workspace build
5. The script uses `rclpy` directly via `python foundationpose_ros_multi.py`
6. All CMakeLists.txt files found are from subcomponents (pybind11, Eigen, mycpp), not ROS2 packages

**How it runs:** The `run_foundationpose.sh` script simply sources ROS2 setup.bash and runs the Python script directly. The ROS2 node is a standalone Python file that imports rclpy.

---

### Environment files:

- **FoundationPose/** `requirements.txt` -- Another 43 packages (FoundationPose's own deps)

### Key installed packages in `foundationpose_ros` env (confirmed running):

| CUDA in env | 13.0 |

| ultralytics | 8.4.51 |
| trimesh | 4.12.2 |

### Note on NumPy conflict:
There is a known **NumPy version conflict** -- PyTorch 2.1.0 requires `numpy<2`, but the environment was observed to have NumPy 2.2.6. The `requirements.txt` correctly specifies `numpy<2`, but during a build attempt, the download failed due to network issues (BrokenPipeError). The `mycuda` CUDA extension build also fails because pip's build isolation cannot see the conda-installed torch.

### Git repos:
- **Main repo:** `https://github.com/ammar-n-abbas/FoundationPoseROS2.git` (up to date, no submodules)
- **FoundationPose/** is a manual git clone of `https://github.com/NVlabs/FoundationPose.git` (not a git submodule)
- **FoundationPose/nvdiffrast/** is a manual git clone of `https://github.com/NVlabs/nvdiffrast.git`
- **FoundationPose/pybind11/** is a manual git clone of pybind11 (v2.10.0)

### Build status:
The mycpp C++ extensions and bundlesdf/mycuda CUDA extensions need to be compiled. The `setup.py` at `FoundationPose/bundlesdf/mycuda/setup.py` was already patched from C++14 to C++17 (as confirmed by reading the file). However, the actual `pip install -e .` for mycuda failed due to pip build isolation not finding torch in the conda environment.

<path>/home/jinwz/FoundationPoseROS2/FoundationPoseROS2/README.md</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: # FoundationPoseROS2: Multi-Object Pose Estimation and Tracking of Novel Objects in ROS2 with RealSense2
2: 
3: <p align="center">
4:   <img src="assets/demo.gif" alt="Demo Video" width="330">
5:   <img src="assets/demo_robot.gif" alt="Robot Demo Video" width="434"><br>
6: </p>
7: 
8: FoundationPoseROS2 is a ROS2-integrated system for 6D object pose estimation and tracking, based on the FoundationPose architecture. It uses RealSense2 with the Segment Anything Model 2 (SAM2) framework for end-to-end, model-based, real-time pose estimation and tracking of novel objects.
9: 
10: It is built on top of [FoundationPose](https://github.com/NVlabs/FoundationPose) and [live-pose](https://github.com/Kaivalya192/live-pose).
11: 
12: The main advantages to the previous repositories
13: 1. ROS2-based real-time framework that works with 8GB GPU
14: 2. SAM2-based automatic segmentation of the objects
15: 3. Multi-object pose estimation and tracking
16: 4. End-to-end assignment of object models with the segmented masks
17: 
18: Furthermore, it provides an interactive GUI for object model-to-mask assignment for end-to-end multi-pose estimation and tracking.
19: 
20: ## Prerequisites
21: 
22: - **Ubuntu (20/22)**
23: - **ROS2 (Foxy/Humble)**
24: - **Minimum 8GB NVIDIA GPU**
25: - **CUDA 12.x**
26: - **Intel RealSense Camera**
27: 
28: 
29: ## Dependencies
30: 
31: ```bash
32: # Install ROS2 on Ubuntu
33: sudo apt install ros-<ROS_DISTRO>-desktop
34: 
35: # Install librealsense2
36: sudo apt install ros-<ROS_DISTRO>-librealsense2*
37: 
38: # Install debian realsense2 package
39: sudo apt install ros-<ROS_DISTRO>-realsense2-*
40: 
41: # Setup CUDA 12.x
42: sudo apt-get --purge remove 'nvidia-*'
43: sudo apt-get autoremove
44: sudo reboot
45: 
46: wget https://developer.download.nvidia.com/compute/cuda/repos/ubuntu2004/x86_64/cuda-keyring_1.0-1_all.deb
47: sudo dpkg -i cuda-keyring_1.0-1_all.deb
48: sudo apt-get update
49: sudo apt-get -y install cuda
50: 
51: # Install Miniconda
52: mkdir -p ~/miniconda3
53: wget https://repo.anaconda.com/miniconda/Miniconda3-latest-Linux-x86_64.sh -O ~/miniconda3/miniconda.sh
54: bash ~/miniconda3/miniconda.sh -b -u -p ~/miniconda3
55: rm ~/miniconda3/miniconda.sh
56: 
57: source ~/miniconda3/bin/activate
58: ```
59: 
60: ## Env setup: conda 
61: 
62: ```bash
63: # Clone repository
64: git clone https://github.com/ammar-n-abbas/FoundationPoseROS2.git
65: ```
66: 
67: ```bash
68: # Create conda environment
69: conda create -n foundationpose_ros python=3.10 -y
70: 
71: # Activate conda environment
72: conda activate foundationpose_ros
73: ```
74: > [!NOTE]
75: > Conda environment must be created with the correct Python version according to the ROS2 distribution to ensure compatibility. For example, use Python 3.8 for ROS Foxy and Python 3.10 for ROS Humble.
76: 
77: ```bash
78: # Build extensions
79: cd FoundationPoseROS2 && export PATH=/usr/local/<YOUR_cuda-12.X_VERSION>/bin${PATH:+:${PATH}}~ && bash build_all_conda.sh
80: ```
81: > [!IMPORTANT]
82: > In the `setup.py` file located at `/FoundationPose/bundlesdf/mycuda/`, the C++ flags should be updated from **C++14** to **C++17** for compatibility with newer Nvidia GPUs. It is located at lines 18 and 19 in the file's `nvcc_flags` and c_flags section.
83: 
84: 
85: ## Run model-based demo
86: 
87: ```bash
88: # Run camera node
89: source /opt/ros/<ROS_DISTRO>/setup.bash && ros2 launch realsense2_camera rs_launch.py enable_rgbd:=true enable_sync:=true align_depth.enable:=true enable_color:=true enable_depth:=true pointcloud.enable:=true
90: ```
91: 
92: In a separate terminal
93: 
94: ```bash
95: # Run foundationpose_ros_multi
96: conda activate foundationpose_ros && source /opt/ros/<ROS_DISTRO>/setup.bash && export PATH=/usr/local/<YOUR_cuda-12.X_VERSION>/bin${PATH:+:${PATH}}~ && python ./FoundationPoseROS2/foundationpose_ros_multi.py
97: ```
98: > [!NOTE]
99: > Check the camera topics and edit accordingly in the Python file.
100: 
101: <div align="center">
102:   <video src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/4ef1f4cf-8900-451d-b006-47942b9f4606" width="200" />
103: </div>
104: 
105: 
106: ## Run model-based demo through Rosbag2
107: 
108: ### 1. Download the Recorded rosbag
109: 
110: First, download the recorded rosbag from the following link:
111: 
112: [Download Recorded rosbag](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/17FRQog_fpqVxo_T85ItfpEa6fUo5fhZ7?usp=sharing)
113: 
114: ### 2. Play the Recorded rosbag
115: 
116: Once you've downloaded the rosbag file, navigate to the directory where it's located, and play it back with the following command:
117: 
118: ```bash
119: # Play the downloaded rosbag
120: source /opt/ros/<ROS_DISTRO>/setup.bash && ros2 bag play cube_demo_data_rosbag2/cube_demo_data_rosbag2.db3
121: ```
122: 
123: Replace `<path_to_your_rosbag_file>` with the path to the `.db3` file you downloaded.
124: 
125: ### 3. Run FoundationPoseROS2
126: 
127: In a separate terminal, activate your conda environment, export the correct CUDA version path and run the FoundationPoseROS2 script to start object pose estimation and tracking:
128: 
129: ```bash
130: # Activate the conda environment and run foundationpose_ros_multi
131: conda activate foundationpose_ros && source /opt/ros/<ROS_DISTRO>/setup.bash && export PATH=/usr/local/<YOUR_cuda-12.X_VERSION>/bin${PATH:+:${PATH}}~ && python ./FoundationPoseROS2/foundationpose_ros_multi.py
132: ```
133: 
134: <p align="center">
135:   <a href="https://youtu.be/p-QBK7yJKWs">
136:     <img src="https://img.youtube.com/vi/p-QBK7yJKWs/0.jpg" alt="Demo data rosbag screen recording">
137:   </a>
138: </p>
139: 
140: [Demo data rosbag terminal log](https://github.com/ammar-n-abbas/FoundationPoseROS2/blob/main/_misc/demo_data_terminal_log.txt)
141: 
142: ## Run on novel objects
143: 
144: Add the mesh file in .obj or .stl format to the folder:
145: ```bash
146: "./FoundationPoseROS2/demo_data/object_name/<OBJECT_MESH>.obj"
147: ```
148: 
149: ```bash
150: # Run
151: conda activate foundationpose_ros && source /opt/ros/<ROS_DISTRO>/setup.bash && export PATH=/usr/local/<YOUR_cuda-12.X_VERSION>/bin${PATH:+:${PATH}}~ && python ./FoundationPoseROS2/foundationpose_ros_multi.py
152: ```
153: 
154: > [!NOTE]
155: > Check the camera topics and edit accordingly in the Python file.
156: 
157: 
158: ## Features
159: 
160: - **Object Selection GUI**: Choose and reorder object files (.obj, .stl) using a simple Tkinter GUI.
161: - **Segmentation and Tracking**: SAM2 is used for object segmentation in real-time colour and depth images from a camera.
162: - **Pose Estimation**: Calculates and publishes the pose of detected objects based on camera images.
163: - **3D Visualization**: Visualize the objects’ pose with bounding boxes and axes.
164: 
165: 
166: ## Pipeline
167: 
168: <p align="center">
169:     <img src="assets/pipeline.svg" alt="Algorithm Pipeline" style="width: 30%; height: auto;"/>
170: </p>
171: 
172: [![Star History Chart](https://api.star-history.com/svg?repos=ammar-n-abbas/FoundationPoseROS2&type=Date)](https://star-history.com/#ammar-n-abbas/FoundationPoseROS2&Date)
173: 
174: ## Acknowledgement
175: This project has emanated from research conducted with the financial support of the EU Commission Recovery and Resilience Facility under the Science Foundation Ireland Future Digital Challenge Grant Number 22/NCF/FD/10929.

</content>

43: class FileSelectorGUI:
44:     def __init__(self, master, file_paths):
45:         self.master = master
46:         self.master.title("Library: Sequence Selector")
47:         self.file_paths = file_paths
48:         self.reordered_paths = None  # Store the reordered paths here
49: 
50:         # Create a listbox to display the file names
51:         self.listbox = Listbox(master, selectmode="extended", width=50, height=10)
52:         self.listbox.pack()
53: 
54:         # Populate the listbox with file names without extensions
55:         for file_path in self.file_paths:
56:             file_name = os.path.splitext(os.path.basename(file_path))[0]
57:             self.listbox.insert(END, file_name)
58: 
59:         # Buttons for rearranging the order
60:         self.up_button = Button(master, text="Move Up", command=self.move_up)
61:         self.up_button.pack(side="left", padx=5, pady=5)
62: 
63:         self.down_button = Button(master, text="Move Down", command=self.move_down)
64:         self.down_button.pack(side="left", padx=5, pady=5)
65: 
66:         self.done_button = Button(master, text="Done", command=self.done)
67:         self.done_button.pack(side="left", padx=5, pady=5)
68: 
69:     def move_up(self):
70:         """Move selected items up in the listbox."""
71:         selected_indices = list(self.listbox.curselection())
72:         for index in selected_indices:
73:             if index > 0:
74:                 # Swap with the previous item
75:                 file_name = self.listbox.get(index)
76:                 self.listbox.delete(index)
77:                 self.listbox.insert(index - 1, file_name)
78:                 self.listbox.selection_set(index - 1)
79: 
80:     def move_down(self):
81:         """Move selected items down in the listbox."""
82:         selected_indices = list(self.listbox.curselection())
83:         for index in reversed(selected_indices):
84:             if index < self.listbox.size() - 1:
85:                 # Swap with the next item
86:                 file_name = self.listbox.get(index)
87:                 self.listbox.delete(index)
88:                 self.listbox.insert(index + 1, file_name)
89:                 self.listbox.selection_set(index + 1)
90: 
91:     def done(self):
92:         """Save the reordered paths and close the GUI."""
93:         reordered_file_names = self.listbox.get(0, END)
94: 
95:         # Recreate the full file paths based on the reordered file names (without extensions)
96:         file_name_to_full_path = {
97:             os.path.splitext(os.path.basename(file))[0]: file for file in self.file_paths
98:         }
99:         self.reordered_paths = [file_name_to_full_path[file_name] for file_name in reordered_file_names]
100: 
101:         # Close the GUI
102:         self.master.quit()
103:

122: class PoseEstimationNode(Node):
123:     def __init__(self, new_file_paths):
124:         super().__init__('pose_estimation_node')
125:         
126:         # ROS subscriptions and publishers
127:         self.image_sub = self.create_subscription(Image, '/camera/camera/color/image_raw', self.image_callback, 10)
128:         self.depth_sub = self.create_subscription(Image, '/camera/camera/aligned_depth_to_color/image_raw', self.depth_callback, 10)
129:         self.info_sub = self.create_subscription(CameraInfo, '/camera/camera/color/camera_info', self.camera_info_callback, 10)
130:         
131:         self.bridge = CvBridge()
132:         self.depth_image = None
133:         self.color_image = None
134:         self.cam_K = None  # Initialize cam_K as None until we receive the camera info
135:         
136:         # Load meshes
137:         self.mesh_files = new_file_paths
138:         self.meshes = [trimesh.load(mesh) for mesh in self.mesh_files]
139:         
140:         self.bounds = [trimesh.bounds.oriented_bounds(mesh) for mesh in self.meshes]
141:         self.bboxes = [np.stack([-extents/2, extents/2], axis=0).reshape(2, 3) for _, extents in self.bounds]
142:         
143:         self.scorer = ScorePredictor()
144:         self.refiner = PoseRefinePredictor()
145:         self.glctx = dr.RasterizeCudaContext()
146: 
147:         # Initialize SAM2 model
148:         self.seg_model = SAM("sam2.1_b.pt")
149: 
150:         self.pose_estimations = {}  # Dictionary to track multiple pose estimations
151:         self.pose_publishers = {}  # Dictionary to store publishers for each object
152:         self.tracked_objects = []  # Initialize to store selected objects' masks
153:         self.i = 0
154: 
155:     def camera_info_callback(self, msg):
156:         if self.cam_K is None:  # Update cam_K only once to avoid redundant updates
157:             self.cam_K = np.array(msg.k).reshape((3, 3))
158:             self.get_logger().info(f"Camera intrinsic matrix initialized: {self.cam_K}")
159: 
160:     def image_callback(self, msg):
161:         self.color_image = self.bridge.imgmsg_to_cv2(msg, "rgb8")
162: 
163:     def depth_callback(self, msg):
164:         self.depth_image = self.bridge.imgmsg_to_cv2(msg, "32FC1") / 1e3
165:         self.process_images()
166: 
167:     def process_images(self):
168:         if self.color_image is None or self.depth_image is None or self.cam_K is None:
169:             return
170: 
171:         H, W = self.color_image.shape[:2]
172:         color = cv2.resize(self.color_image, (W, H), interpolation=cv2.INTER_NEAREST)
173:         depth = cv2.resize(self.depth_image, (W, H), interpolation=cv2.INTER_NEAREST)
174:         depth[(depth < 0.1) | (depth >= np.inf)] = 0
175: 
176:         if self.i == 0:
177:             masks_accepted = False
178: 
179:             while not masks_accepted:
180:                 # Use SAM2 for segmentation
181:                 res = self.seg_model.predict(color)[0]
182:                 res.save("masks.png")
183:                 if not res:
184:                     self.get_logger().warn("No masks detected by SAM2.")
185:                     return
186: 
187:                 objects_to_track = []
188: 
189:                 # Iterate over the segmentation results to extract the masks and bounding boxes
190:                 for r in res:
191:                     img = np.copy(r.orig_img)
192:                     for ci, c in enumerate(r):
193:                         mask = np.zeros((H, W), np.uint8)
194:                         contour = c.masks.xy.pop().astype(np.int32).reshape(-1, 1, 2)
195:                         _ = cv2.drawContours(mask, [contour], -1, (255, 255, 255), cv2.FILLED)
196: 
197:                         # Store mask and bounding box
198:                         objects_to_track.append({
199:                             'mask': mask,
200:                             'box': c.boxes.xyxy.tolist().pop(),
201:                             'contour': contour
202:                         })
203: 
204:                 if not objects_to_track:
205:                     self.get_logger().warn("No objects found in the image.")
206:                     return
207: 
208:                 self.tracked_objects = []  # Reset tracked objects for redo
209:                 temporary_pose_estimations = {}
210:                 skipped_indices = []  # Track skipped objects' indices
211: 
212:                 def click_event(event, x, y, flags, params):
213:                     if event == cv2.EVENT_LBUTTONDOWN:
214:                         closest_dist = float('inf')
215:                         selected_obj = None
216: 
217:                         for obj in objects_to_track:
218:                             if obj['mask'][y, x] == 255:  # Check if click is inside the mask
219:                                 dist = cv2.pointPolygonTest(obj['contour'], (x, y), True)
220: 
221:                                 if dist < closest_dist:
222:                                     closest_dist = dist
223:                                     selected_obj = obj
224: 
225:                         if selected_obj is not None:
226:                             sequential_id = len(self.tracked_objects) + len(skipped_indices)
227:                             self.get_logger().info(f"Object {sequential_id} selected.")
228:                             self.tracked_objects.append(selected_obj['mask'])
229: 
230:                             # Temporarily store the mesh and bounds to avoid permanent removal
231:                             temp_mesh = self.meshes.pop(0)  # Remove the first mesh in line
232:                             temp_to_origin, _ = self.bounds.pop(0)  # Remove the first bound in line
233: 
234:                             # Initialize FoundationPose for each detected object with corresponding mesh
235:                             pose_est = FoundationPose(
236:                                 model_pts=temp_mesh.vertices,
237:                                 model_normals=temp_mesh.vertex_normals,
238:                                 mesh=temp_mesh,
239:                                 scorer=self.scorer,
240:                                 refiner=self.refiner,
241:                                 glctx=self.glctx
242:                             )
243: 
244:                             temporary_pose_estimations[sequential_id] = {
245:                                 'pose_est': pose_est,
246:                                 'mask': selected_obj['mask'],
247:                                 'to_origin': temp_to_origin
248:                             }
249: 
250:                             # Refresh the dialog box with the updated object name
251:                             refresh_dialog_box()
252: 
253:                 def refresh_dialog_box():
254:                     # Display contours for all detected objects
255:                     combined_mask_image = np.copy(color)
256:                     for idx, obj in enumerate(objects_to_track):
257:                         cv2.drawContours(combined_mask_image, [obj['contour']], -1, (0, 255, 0), 2)  # Green contours
258: 
259:                     # Get the next mesh name for user guidance, accounting for skips
260:                     next_mesh_idx = len(self.tracked_objects) + len(skipped_indices)
261:                     if next_mesh_idx < len(self.mesh_files):
262:                         next_mesh_name = os.path.basename(self.mesh_files[next_mesh_idx].split("/")[-1].split(".")[0])
263:                     else:
264:                         next_mesh_name = "None"
265: 
266:                     # Create the dialog box overlay
267:                     overlay = combined_mask_image.copy()
268:                     dialog_text = (
269:                         f"Next object to select: {next_mesh_name}\n"
270:                         "Instructions:\n"
271:                         "- Click on the object to select.\n"
272:                         "- Press 's' to skip the current object.\n"
273:                         "- Press 'c', 'Enter', or 'Space' to confirm selection.\n"
274:                         "- Press 'r' to redo mask selection.\n"
275:                         "- Press 'q' to quit.\n"
276:                     )
277:                     y0, dy = 30, 20
278:                     for i, line in enumerate(dialog_text.split('\n')):
279:                         y = y0 + i * dy
280:                         cv2.putText(overlay, line, (10, y), cv2.FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX, 0.5, (255, 255, 255), 1, cv2.LINE_AA)
281: 
282:                     cv2.imshow('Click on objects to track', overlay)
283:                     cv2.setMouseCallback('Click on objects to track', click_event)
284: 
285:                 refresh_dialog_box()
286: 
287:                 while True:
288:                     key = cv2.waitKey(0)  # Wait for a key event
289:                     if key == ord('r'):
290:                         self.get_logger().info("Redoing mask selection.")
291:                         break  # Break the inner loop to redo mask selection
292:                     elif key == ord('s'):
293:                         self.get_logger().info("Skipping current object.")
294:                         skipped_indices.append(len(self.tracked_objects) + len(skipped_indices))  # Track skipped mesh index
295: 
296:                         # Remove the first mesh and bounds in line
297:                         self.meshes.pop(0)
298:                         self.bounds.pop(0)
299: 
300:                         refresh_dialog_box()
301:                     elif key in [ord('q'), 27]:  # 'q' or Esc to quit
302:                         self.get_logger().info("Quitting mask selection.")
303:                         return
304:                     elif key in [ord('c'), 13, 32]:  # 'c', Enter, or Space to confirm
305:                         if self.tracked_objects:
306:                             # Confirm the selection and update the actual pose_estimations
307:                             self.pose_estimations = temporary_pose_estimations
308: 
309:                             # Remove the corresponding meshes and bounds from the original lists only after confirmation
310:                             selected_indices = sorted(temporary_pose_estimations.keys(), reverse=True)
311:                             self.meshes = [self.meshes[idx] for idx in selected_indices]
312:                             self.bounds = [self.bounds[idx] for idx in selected_indices]
313: 
314:                             masks_accepted = True  # Exit the outer loop if masks are accepted
315:                             break
316:                         else:
317:                             self.get_logger().warn("No objects selected. Redo mask selection.")
318: 
319:         visualization_image = np.copy(color)
320: 
321:         for idx, data in self.pose_estimations.items():
322:             pose_est = data['pose_est']
323:             obj_mask = data['mask']
324:             to_origin = data['to_origin']
325:             if pose_est.is_register:
326:                 pose = pose_est.track_one(rgb=color, depth=depth, K=self.cam_K, iteration=args.track_refine_iter)
327:                 center_pose = pose @ np.linalg.inv(to_origin)
328: 
329:                 self.publish_pose_stamped(center_pose, f"object_{idx}_frame", f"/Current_OBJ_position_{idx+1}")
330: 
331:                 visualization_image = self.visualize_pose(visualization_image, center_pose, idx)
332:             else:
333:                 pose = pose_est.register(K=self.cam_K, rgb=color, depth=depth, ob_mask=obj_mask, iteration=args.est_refine_iter)
334:             self.i += 1
335: 
336:         cv2.imshow('Pose Estimation & Tracking', visualization_image[..., ::-1])
337:         cv2.waitKey(1)
338: 
339:     def visualize_pose(self, image, center_pose, idx):
340:         bbox = self.bboxes[idx % len(self.bboxes)]
341:         vis = draw_posed_3d_box(self.cam_K, img=image, ob_in_cam=center_pose, bbox=bbox)
342:         vis = draw_xyz_axis(vis, ob_in_cam=center_pose, scale=0.1, K=self.cam_K, thickness=3, transparency=0, is_input_rgb=True)
343:         return vis
344: 
345:     def publish_pose_stamped(self, center_pose, frame_id, topic_name):
346:         if topic_name not in self.pose_publishers:
347:             self.pose_publishers[topic_name] = self.create_publisher(PoseStamped, topic_name, 10)
348:         
349:         # Convert the center_pose matrix to a PoseStamped message
350:         pose_stamped_msg = PoseStamped()
351:         pose_stamped_msg.header.stamp = self.get_clock().now().to_msg()
352:         pose_stamped_msg.header.frame_id = frame_id
353: 
354:         # Convert center_pose to the pose format
355:         position = center_pose[:3, 3]
356:         rotation_matrix = center_pose[:3, :3]
357:         quaternion = R.from_matrix(rotation_matrix).as_quat()
358: 
359:         # Combine position and quaternion into a single array
360:         pose_array = np.concatenate((position, quaternion))
361: 
362:         # Apply transformation to convert from camera to base frame
363:         transformed_pose = transformation(pose_array)
364: 
365:         # Populate PoseStamped message with transformed pose
366:         pose_stamped_msg.pose.position.x = transformed_pose[0]
367:         pose_stamped_msg.pose.position.y = transformed_pose[1]
368:         pose_stamped_msg.pose.position.z = transformed_pose[2]
369: 
370:         pose_stamped_msg.pose.orientation.w = transformed_pose[3]
371:         pose_stamped_msg.pose.orientation.x = transformed_pose[4]
372:         pose_stamped_msg.pose.orientation.y = transformed_pose[5]
373:         pose_stamped_msg.pose.orientation.z = transformed_pose[6]
374: 
375:         # Publish the transformed pose
376:         self.pose_publishers[topic_name].publish(pose_stamped_msg)
377: 
378: def main(args=None):
379:     source_directory = "demo_data"
380:     file_paths = glob.glob(os.path.join(source_directory, '**', '*.obj'), recursive=True) + \
381:                  glob.glob(os.path.join(source_directory, '**', '*.stl'), recursive=True) + \
382:                  glob.glob(os.path.join(source_directory, '**', '*.STL'), recursive=True)
383: 
384:     # Call the function to rearrange files through the GUI
385:     new_file_paths = rearrange_files(file_paths)
386: 
387:     rclpy.init(args=args)
388:     node = PoseEstimationNode(new_file_paths)
389:     rclpy.spin(node)
390:     node.destroy_node()
391:     rclpy.shutdown()
392: 
393: if __name__ == '__main__':
394:     main()

(End of file - total 394 lines)
</content>
```

1: #!/bin/bash
2: 
3: PROJ_ROOT=$( cd -- "$( dirname -- "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}" )" &> /dev/null && pwd )
4: 
5: # Install dependencies
6: pip install torchvision==0.16.0+cu121 torchaudio==2.1.0 torch==2.1.0+cu121 --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu121
7: pip install "git+https://github.com/facebookresearch/pytorch3d.git@stable"
8: python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
9: 
10: # Clone source repository of FoundationPose
11: git clone https://github.com/NVlabs/FoundationPose.git
12: 
13: # Create the weights directory and download the pretrained weights from FoundationPose
14: gdown --folder https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1BEQLZH69UO5EOfah-K9bfI3JyP9Hf7wC -O FoundationPose/weights/2023-10-28-18-33-37 
15: gdown --folder https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/12Te_3TELLes5cim1d7F7EBTwUSe7iRBj -O FoundationPose/weights/2024-01-11-20-02-45
16: 
17: # Install pybind11
18: cd ${PROJ_ROOT}/FoundationPose && git clone https://github.com/pybind/pybind11 && \
19:     cd pybind11 && git checkout v2.10.0 && \
20:     mkdir build && cd build && cmake .. -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release -DPYBIND11_INSTALL=ON -DPYBIND11_TEST=OFF && \
21:     sudo make -j6 && sudo make install
22: 
23: # Install Eigen
24: cd ${PROJ_ROOT}/FoundationPose && wget https://gitlab.com/libeigen/eigen/-/archive/3.4.0/eigen-3.4.0.tar.gz && \
25:     tar xvzf ./eigen-3.4.0.tar.gz && rm ./eigen-3.4.0.tar.gz && \
26:     cd eigen-3.4.0 && \
27:     mkdir build && \
28:     cd build && \
29:     cmake .. && \
30:     sudo make install
31: 
32: # Clone and install nvdiffrast
33: cd ${PROJ_ROOT}/FoundationPose && git clone https://github.com/NVlabs/nvdiffrast && \
34:     cd /nvdiffrast && pip install .
35: 
36: # Install mycpp
37: cd ${PROJ_ROOT}/FoundationPose/mycpp/ && \
38: rm -rf build && mkdir -p build && cd build && \
39: cmake .. && \
40: sudo make -j$(nproc)
41: 
42: # Install mycuda
43: cd ${PROJ_ROOT}/FoundationPose/bundlesdf/mycuda && \
44: rm -rf build *egg* *.so && \
45: python3 -m pip install -e .
46: 
47: cd ${PROJ_ROOT}

<type>file</type>
<content>
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: from geometry_msgs.msg import Pose, Point, Quaternion, PoseArray, PoseStamped, PointStamped, TransformStamped
3: from std_msgs.msg import Header
4: import numpy
5: # import rospy
6: from scipy.spatial.transform import Rotation as Rot
7: import math
8: 
9: _EPS = numpy.finfo(float).eps * 4.0
10: 
11: def quaternion_matrix(quaternion):
12:     """Return homogeneous rotation matrix from quaternion.
13: 
14:     >>> R = quaternion_matrix([0.06146124, 0, 0, 0.99810947])
15:     >>> numpy.allclose(R, rotation_matrix(0.123, (1, 0, 0)))
16:     True
17: 
18:     """
19:     q = numpy.array(quaternion[:4], dtype=numpy.float64, copy=True)
20:     nq = numpy.dot(q, q)
21:     if nq < _EPS:
22:         return numpy.identity(4)
23:     q *= math.sqrt(2.0 / nq)
24:     q = numpy.outer(q, q)
25:     return numpy.array((
26:         (1.0-q[1, 1]-q[2, 2],     q[0, 1]-q[2, 3],     q[0, 2]+q[1, 3], 0.0),
27:         (    q[0, 1]+q[2, 3], 1.0-q[0, 0]-q[2, 2],     q[1, 2]-q[0, 3], 0.0),
28:         (    q[0, 2]-q[1, 3],     q[1, 2]+q[0, 3], 1.0-q[0, 0]-q[1, 1], 0.0),
29:         (                0.0,                 0.0,                 0.0, 1.0)
30:         ), dtype=numpy.float64)
31: 
32: def quaternion_from_matrix(matrix):
33:     """Return quaternion from rotation matrix.
34: 
35:     >>> R = rotation_matrix(0.123, (1, 2, 3))
36:     >>> q = quaternion_from_matrix(R)
37:     >>> numpy.allclose(q, [0.0164262, 0.0328524, 0.0492786, 0.9981095])
38:     True
39: 
40:     """
41:     q = numpy.empty((4, ), dtype=numpy.float64)
42:     M = numpy.array(matrix, dtype=numpy.float64, copy=False)[:4, :4]
43:     t = numpy.trace(M)
44:     if t > M[3, 3]:
45:         q[3] = t
46:         q[2] = M[1, 0] - M[0, 1]
47:         q[1] = M[0, 2] - M[2, 0]
48:         q[0] = M[2, 1] - M[1, 2]
49:     else:
50:         i, j, k = 0, 1, 2
51:         if M[1, 1] > M[0, 0]:
52:             i, j, k = 1, 2, 0
53:         if M[2, 2] > M[i, i]:
54:             i, j, k = 2, 0, 1
55:         t = M[i, i] - (M[j, j] + M[k, k]) + M[3, 3]
56:         q[i] = t
57:         q[j] = M[i, j] + M[j, i]
58:         q[k] = M[k, i] + M[i, k]
59:         q[3] = M[k, j] - M[j, k]
60:     q *= 0.5 / math.sqrt(t * M[3, 3])
61:     return q
62: 
63: def PoseStamped_2_mat(p):
64:     q = p.pose.orientation
65:     pos = p.pose.position
66:     T = quaternion_matrix([q.x,q.y,q.z,q.w])
67:     T[:3,3] = numpy.array([pos.x,pos.y,pos.z])
68:     return T
69: 
70: def Pose_2_mat(p):
71:     q = p.orientation
72:     pos = p.position
73:     T = quaternion_matrix([q.x,q.y,q.z,q.w])
74:     T[:3,3] = numpy.array([pos.x,pos.y,pos.z])
75:     return T
76: 
77: def Mat_2_posestamped(m,f_id="test"):
78:     q = quaternion_from_matrix(m)
79:     p = PoseStamped(header = Header(frame_id=f_id), #robot.get_planning_frame()
80:                     pose=Pose(position=Point(*m[:3,3]), 
81:                     orientation=Quaternion(*q)))
82:     return p
83: 
84: def T_inv(T_in):
85:     R_in = T_in[:3,:3]
86:     t_in = T_in[:3,[-1]]
87:     R_out = R_in.T
88:     t_out = -numpy.matmul(R_out,t_in)
89:     return numpy.vstack((numpy.hstack((R_out,t_out)),numpy.array([0, 0, 0, 1])))
90: 
91: def transformation(pose):
92:     p_OwrtC=Pose() 
93:     p_OwrtC.position.x=float(pose[0])
94:     p_OwrtC.position.y=float(pose[1])
95:     p_OwrtC.position.z=float(pose[2])
96:     p_OwrtC.orientation.w=float(pose[6])
97:     p_OwrtC.orientation.x=float(pose[3])
98:     p_OwrtC.orientation.y=float(pose[4])
99:     p_OwrtC.orientation.z=float(pose[5])
100:     Tco = Pose_2_mat(p_OwrtC)
101: 
102:     p_CwrtB=Pose() 
103:     p_CwrtB.position.x=-0.143361
104:     p_CwrtB.position.y=-1.45842
105:     p_CwrtB.position.z=0.375607
106:     p_CwrtB.orientation.w=0.575573
107:     p_CwrtB.orientation.x=-0.817741
108:     p_CwrtB.orientation.y=-0.00388839
109:     p_CwrtB.orientation.z=-0.000290818
110:     Tbc = Pose_2_mat(p_CwrtB)
111: 
112:     Tbo = numpy.matmul(Tbc, Tco)
113: 
114:     rot_mat1 = Rot.from_matrix(Tbo[:3,:3])
115:     quat1 = rot_mat1.as_quat()
116:     rot_vec1 = rot_mat1.as_rotvec()
117:     position1 = Tbo[:3,3]
118: 
119:     pose= numpy.concatenate((position1, quat1))
120:     object_pose_base = [pose[0], pose[1], pose[2], pose[6], pose[3], pose[4], pose[5]] #wxyz
121:     return object_pose_base

(End of file - total 121 lines)
</content>

116: 3: <p align="center">
117: 4:   <img src="assets/demo.gif" alt="Demo Video" width="330">
118: 5:   <img src="assets/demo_robot.gif" alt="Robot Demo Video" width="434"><br>
119: 6: </p>
120: 7: 
121: 8: FoundationPoseROS2 is a ROS2-integrated system for 6D object pose estimation and tracking, based on the FoundationPose architecture. It uses RealSense2 with the Segment Anything Model 2 (SAM2) framework for end-to-end, model-based, real-time pose estimation and tracking of novel objects.
122: 9: 
123: 10: It is built on top of [FoundationPose](https://github.com/NVlabs/FoundationPose) and [live-pose](https://github.com/Kaivalya192/live-pose).
124: 11: 
125: 12: The main advantages to the previous repositories
126: 13: 1. ROS2-based real-time framework that works with 8GB GPU
127: 14: 2. SAM2-based automatic segmentation of the objects
128: 15: 3. Multi-object pose estimation and tracking
129: 16: 4. End-to-end assignment of object models with the segmented masks
130: 17: 
131: 18: Furthermore, it provides an interactive GUI for object model-to-mask assignment for end-to-end multi-pose estimation and tracking.
132: 19: 
133: 20: ## Prerequisites
134: 21: 
135: 22: - **Ubuntu (20/22)**
136: 23: - **ROS2 (Foxy/Humble)**
137: 24: - **Minimum 8GB NVIDIA GPU**
138: 25: - **CUDA 12.x**
139: 26: - **Intel RealSense Camera**
140: 27: 
141: 28: 
142: 29: ## Dependencies
143: 30: 
144: 31: ```bash
145: 32: # Install ROS2 on Ubuntu
146: 33: sudo apt install ros-<ROS_DISTRO>-desktop
147: 34: 
148: 35: # Install librealsense2
149: 36: sudo apt install ros-<ROS_DISTRO>-librealsense2*
150: 37: 
151: 38: # Install debian realsense2 package
152: 39: sudo apt install ros-<ROS_DISTRO>-realsense2-*
153: 40: 
154: 41: # Setup CUDA 12.x
155: 42: sudo apt-get --purge remove 'nvidia-*'
156: 43: sudo apt-get autoremove
157: 44: sudo reboot
158: 45: 
159: 46: wget https://developer.download.nvidia.com/compute/cuda/repos/ubuntu2004/x86_64/cuda-keyring_1.0-1_all.deb
160: 47: sudo dpkg -i cuda-keyring_1.0-1_all.deb
161: 48: sudo apt-get update
162: 49: sudo apt-get -y install cuda
163: 50: 
164: 51: # Install Miniconda
165: 52: mkdir -p ~/miniconda3
166: 53: wget https://repo.anaconda.com/miniconda/Miniconda3-latest-Linux-x86_64.sh -O ~/miniconda3/miniconda.sh
167: 54: bash ~/miniconda3/miniconda.sh -b -u -p ~/miniconda3
168: 55: rm ~/miniconda3/miniconda.sh
169: 56: 
170: 57: source ~/miniconda3/bin/activate
171: 58: ```
172: 59: 
173: 60: ## Env setup: conda 
174: 61: 
175: 62: ```bash
176: 63: # Clone repository
177: 64: git clone https://github.com/ammar-n-abbas/FoundationPoseROS2.git
178: 65: ```
179: 66: 
180: 67: ```bash
181: 68: # Create conda environment
182: 69: conda create -n foundationpose_ros python=3.10 -y
183: 70: 
184: 71: # Activate conda environment
185: 72: conda activate foundationpose_ros
186: 73: ```
187: 74: > [!NOTE]
188: 75: > Conda environment must be created with the correct Python version according to the ROS2 distribution to ensure compatibility. For example, use Python 3.8 for ROS Foxy and Python 3.10 for ROS Humble.
189: 76: 
190: 77: ```bash
191: 78: # Build extensions
192: 79: cd FoundationPoseROS2 && export PATH=/usr/local/<YOUR_cuda-12.X_VERSION>/bin${PATH:+:${PATH}}~ && bash build_all_conda.sh
193: 80: ```
194: 81: > [!IMPORTANT]
195: 82: > In the `setup.py` file located at `/FoundationPose/bundlesdf/mycuda/`, the C++ flags should be updated from **C++14** to **C++17** for compatibility with newer Nvidia GPUs. It is located at lines 18 and 19 in the file's `nvcc_flags` and c_flags section.
196: 83: 
197: 84: 
198: 85: ## Run model-based demo
199: 86: 
200: 87: ```bash
201: 88: # Run camera node
202: 89: source /opt/ros/<ROS_DISTRO>/setup.bash && ros2 launch realsense2_camera rs_launch.py enable_rgbd:=true enable_sync:=true align_depth.enable:=true enable_color:=true enable_depth:=true pointcloud.enable:=true
203: 90: ```
204: 91: 
205: 92: In a separate terminal
206: 93: 
207: 94: ```bash
208: 95: # Run foundationpose_ros_multi
209: 96: conda activate foundationpose_ros && source /opt/ros/<ROS_DISTRO>/setup.bash && export PATH=/usr/local/<YOUR_cuda-12.X_VERSION>/bin${PATH:+:${PATH}}~ && python ./FoundationPoseROS2/foundationpose_ros_multi.py
210: 97: ```
211: 98: > [!NOTE]
212: 99: > Check the camera topics and edit accordingly in the Python file.
213: 100: 
214: 101: <div align="center">
215: 102:   <video src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/4ef1f4cf-8900-451d-b006-47942b9f4606" width="200" />
216: 103: </div>
217: 104: 
218: 105: 
219: 106: ## Run model-based demo through Rosbag2
220: 107: 
221: 108: ### 1. Download the Recorded rosbag
222: 109: 
223: 110: First, download the recorded rosbag from the following link:
224: 111: 
225: 112: [Download Recorded rosbag](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/17FRQog_fpqVxo_T85ItfpEa6fUo5fhZ7?usp=sharing)
226: 113: 
227: 114: ### 2. Play the Recorded rosbag
228: 115: 
229: 116: Once you've downloaded the rosbag file, navigate to the directory where it's located, and play it back with the following command:
230: 117: 
231: 118: ```bash
232: 119: # Play the downloaded rosbag
233: 120: source /opt/ros/<ROS_DISTRO>/setup.bash && ros2 bag play cube_demo_data_rosbag2/cube_demo_data_rosbag2.db3
234: 121: ```
235: 122: 
236: 123: Replace `<path_to_your_rosbag_file>` with the path to the `.db3` file you downloaded.
237: 124: 
238: 125: ### 3. Run FoundationPoseROS2
239: 126: 
240: 127: In a separate terminal, activate your conda environment, export the correct CUDA version path and run the FoundationPoseROS2 script to start object pose estimation and tracking:
241: 128: 
242: 129: ```bash
243: 130: # Activate the conda environment and run foundationpose_ros_multi
244: 131: conda activate foundationpose_ros && source /opt/ros/<ROS_DISTRO>/setup.bash && export PATH=/usr/local/<YOUR_cuda-12.X_VERSION>/bin${PATH:+:${PATH}}~ && python ./FoundationPoseROS2/foundationpose_ros_multi.py
245: 132: ```
246: 133: 
247: 134: <p align="center">
248: 135:   <a href="https://youtu.be/p-QBK7yJKWs">
249: 136:     <img src="https://img.youtube.com/vi/p-QBK7yJKWs/0.jpg" alt="Demo data rosbag screen recording">
250: 137:   </a>
251: 138: </p>
252: 139: 
253: 140: [Demo data rosbag terminal log](https://github.com/ammar-n-abbas/FoundationPoseROS2/blob/main/_misc/demo_data_terminal_log.txt)
254: 141: 
255: 142: ## Run on novel objects
256: 143: 
257: 144: Add the mesh file in .obj or .stl format to the folder:
258: 145: ```bash
259: 146: "./FoundationPoseROS2/demo_data/object_name/<OBJECT_MESH>.obj"
260: 147: ```
261: 148: 
262: 149: ```bash
263: 150: # Run
264: 151: conda activate foundationpose_ros && source /opt/ros/<ROS_DISTRO>/setup.bash && export PATH=/usr/local/<YOUR_cuda-12.X_VERSION>/bin${PATH:+:${PATH}}~ && python ./FoundationPoseROS2/foundationpose_ros_multi.py
265: 152: ```
266: 153: 
267: 154: > [!NOTE]
268: 155: > Check the camera topics and edit accordingly in the Python file.
269: 156: 
270: 157: 
271: 158: ## Features
272: 159: 
273: 160: - **Object Selection GUI**: Choose and reorder object files (.obj, .stl) using a simple Tkinter GUI.
274: 161: - **Segmentation and Tracking**: SAM2 is used for object segmentation in real-time colour and depth images from a camera.
275: 162: - **Pose Estimation**: Calculates and publishes the pose of detected objects based on camera images.
276: 163: - **3D Visualization**: Visualize the objects’ pose with bounding boxes and axes.
277: 164: 
278: 165: 
279: 166: ## Pipeline
280: 167: 
281: 168: <p align="center">
282: 169:     <img src="assets/pipeline.svg" alt="Algorithm Pipeline" style="width: 30%; height: auto;"/>
283: 170: </p>

286: 173: 
287: 174: ## Acknowledgement
288: 175: This project has emanated from research conducted with the financial support of the EU Commission Recovery and Resilience Facility under the Science Foundation Ireland Future Digital Challenge Grant Number 22/NCF/FD/10929.
289:

1263: 60: ## Env setup: conda 
1264: 61: 
1265: 62: ```bash
1266: 63: # Clone repository
1267: 64: git clone https://github.com/ammar-n-abbas/FoundationPoseROS2.git
1268: 65: ```
1269: 66: 
1270: 67: ```bash
1271: 68: # Create conda environment
1272: 69: conda create -n foundationpose_ros python=3.10 -y
1273: 70: 
1274: 71: # Activate conda environment
1275: 72: conda activate foundationpose_ros
1276: 73: ```
1277: 74: > [!NOTE]
1278: 75: > Conda environment must be created with the correct Python version according to the ROS2 distribution to ensure compatibility. For example, use Python 3.8 for ROS Foxy and Python 3.10 for ROS Humble.
1279: 76: 
1280: 77: ```bash
1281: 78: # Build extensions
1282: 79: cd FoundationPoseROS2 && export PATH=/usr/local/<YOUR_cuda-12.X_VERSION>/bin${PATH:+:${PATH}}~ && bash build_all_conda.sh
1283: 80: ```
1284: 81: > [!IMPORTANT]
1285: 82: > In the `setup.py` file located at `/FoundationPose/bundlesdf/mycuda/`, the C++ flags should be updated from **C++14** to **C++17** for compatibility with newer Nvidia GPUs. It is located at lines 18 and 19 in the file's `nvcc_flags` and c_flags section.
1286: 83: 
1287: 84: 
1288: 85: ## Run model-based demo
1289: 86: 
1290: 87: ```bash
1291: 88: # Run camera node
1292: 89: source /opt/ros/<ROS_DISTRO>/setup.bash && ros2 launch realsense2_camera rs_launch.py enable_rgbd:=true enable_sync:=true align_depth.enable:=true enable_color:=true enable_depth:=true pointcloud.enable:=true

1300: ## Assistant (Build · MiniMax M2.5 Free · 7.1s)
1301: 
1302: _Thinking:_
1303: 
1304: 好的，现在我明白了。这是关于conda环境的设置步骤。让我给用户解释这些步骤的含义。
1305: 
1306: 
1307: 
1308: 
1309: "Env setup: conda" 是用Conda创建隔离的Python环境，分3步：
1310: 
1311: 1. **创建环境** - 新建名为 `foundationpose_ros` 的独立环境，Python 3.10（适配ROS2 Humble）
1312: 2. **激活环境** - 切换到该环境，后续所有操作在此环境中执行
1313: 3. **构建扩展** - 运行 `build_all_conda.sh` 编译C++扩展（FoundationPose依赖的底层库）
1314: 
1315: 你已完成了这些步骤，环境 `foundationpose_ros` 已存在。
1316: 
1317: 要验证当前是否在此环境中运行：
1318: 
1319: 
1320: **Tool: bash**
1321: 
1322: **Input:**
1323: ```json
1324: {
1325:   "command": "conda info --envs | grep -E \"^\\*|foundationpose\"",
1326:   "description": "Check current conda env"
1327: }
1328: ```
1329:

1452: 
1453: ## Assistant (Build · MiniMax M2.5 Free · 4.3s)
1454: 
1455: _Thinking:_
1456: 
1457: 用户问的是关于conda的基本概念和为什么要用conda环境。这是一个技术解释问题，不需要用工具，只需要直接回答。
1458: 
1459: 
1460: 
1461: 
1462: **Conda** 是一个Python环境管理工具（类似pip但更强大）。
1463: 
1464: **为什么需要conda环境？**
1465: 
1466: 1. **隔离依赖** - 不同项目可能需要不同版本的库（如PyTorch 2.0 vs 2.4），conda可避免版本冲突
1467: 
1468: 2. **系统兼容性** - FoundationPose依赖特定版本的CUDA、PyTorch、OpenCV等，conda可统一管理这些版本
1469: 
1470: 3. **避免污染系统** - 不把库装到系统Python，保持系统干净
1471: 
1472: 4. **可移植性** - conda环境可导出为配置文件 `.yml`，在其他机器上一键重建相同环境
1473: 
1474: 简单说：把项目需要的Python库"打包"到一个独立空间，互不干扰。
1475:

79: 
80: 
81: 
82: 
83: "Env setup: conda" 是用Conda创建隔离的Python环境，分3步：
84: 
85: 1. **创建环境** - 新建名为 `foundationpose_ros` 的独立环境，Python 3.10（适配ROS2 Humble）
86: 2. **激活环境** - 切换到该环境，后续所有操作在此环境中执行
87: 3. **构建扩展** - 运行 `build_all_conda.sh` 编译C++扩展（FoundationPose依赖的底层库）
88: 
89: 你已完成了这些步骤，环境 `foundationpose_ros` 已存在。
90: 
91: 要验证当前是否在此环境中运行：

224: 
225: ---
226: 
227: ## Assistant (Build · MiniMax M2.5 Free · 4.3s)
228: 
229: _Thinking:_
230: 
231: 用户问的是关于conda的基本概念和为什么要用conda环境。这是一个技术解释问题，不需要用工具，只需要直接回答。
232: 
233: 
234: 
235: 
236: **Conda** 是一个Python环境管理工具（类似pip但更强大）。
237: 
238: **为什么需要conda环境？**
239: 
240: 1. **隔离依赖** - 不同项目可能需要不同版本的库（如PyTorch 2.0 vs 2.4），conda可避免版本冲突
241: 
242: 2. **系统兼容性** - FoundationPose依赖特定版本的CUDA、PyTorch、OpenCV等，conda可统一管理这些版本
243: 
244: 3. **避免污染系统** - 不把库装到系统Python，保持系统干净
245: 
246: 4. **可移植性** - conda环境可导出为配置文件 `.yml`，在其他机器上一键重建相同环境
247: 
248: 简单说：把项目需要的Python库"打包"到一个独立空间，互不干扰。
249:

379: ```bash
380: export PATH=/usr/local/<YOUR_cuda-12.X_VERSION>/bin${PATH:+:${PATH}}~

383: - `export PATH=...` - 把CUDA的bin目录加入系统PATH，让系统能找到`nvcc`编译工具
384: - `<YOUR_cuda-12.X_VERSION>` - 需替换成你的CUDA版本号（如cuda-12.1）

1275:

1278: 
1279: 分两步运行：
1280: 
1281: **第一步：启动相机节点**
1282: ```bash
1283: ros2 launch realsense2_camera rs_launch.py enable_rgbd:=true enable_sync:=true align_depth.enable:=true enable_color:=true enable_depth:=true pointcloud.enable:=true
1284: ```
1285: - 启动Intel RealSense相机
1286: - 启用RGB和深度图像
1287: - 同步RGB和深度
1288: - 对齐深度图到RGB
1289: - 启用点云输出
1290: - 在终端输出相机图像topics（如 `/camera/color/image_raw` 等）
1291: 
1292: **第二步：运行姿态估计程序**
1293: ```bash
1294: python ./FoundationPoseROS2/foundationpose_ros_multi.py
1295: ```
1296: - 订阅相机发布的图像topics
1297: - 加载demo_data/中的物体3D模型(.obj)
1298: - 使用SAM2分割物体
1299: - 估计6D位姿并发布
1300: - 显示GUI让你选择要追踪的物体

<type>file</type>
<content>
1: # FoundationPose: Unified 6D Pose Estimation and Tracking of Novel Objects
2: [[Paper]](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.08344) [[Website]](https://nvlabs.github.io/FoundationPose/)
3: 
4: This is the official implementation of our paper to be appeared in CVPR 2024 (Highlight)
5: 
6: Contributors: Bowen Wen, Wei Yang, Jan Kautz, Stan Birchfield
7: 
8: We present FoundationPose, a unified foundation model for 6D object pose estimation and tracking, supporting both model-based and model-free setups. Our approach can be instantly applied at test-time to a novel object without fine-tuning, as long as its CAD model is given, or a small number of reference images are captured. We bridge the gap between these two setups with a neural implicit representation that allows for effective novel view synthesis, keeping the downstream pose estimation modules invariant under the same unified framework. Strong generalizability is achieved via large-scale synthetic training, aided by a large language model (LLM), a novel transformer-based architecture, and contrastive learning formulation. Extensive evaluation on multiple public datasets involving challenging scenarios and objects indicate our unified approach outperforms existing methods specialized for each task by a large margin. In addition, it even achieves comparable results to instance-level methods despite the reduced assumptions.
9: 
10: 
11: <img src="assets/intro.jpg" width="70%">
12: 
13: **🤖 For ROS version, please check [Isaac ROS Pose Estimation](https://github.com/NVIDIA-ISAAC-ROS/isaac_ros_pose_estimation), which enjoys TRT fast inference and C++ speed up.**
14: 
15: \
16: **🥇 No. 1 on the world-wide [BOP leaderboard](https://bop.felk.cvut.cz/leaderboards/pose-estimation-unseen-bop23/core-datasets/) (as of 2024/03) for model-based novel object pose estimation.**
17: <img src="assets/bop.jpg" width="80%">
18: 
19: ## Demos
20:

22:

24:

32:

34: 
35:

37: # Bibtex

40: author        = {Bowen Wen, Wei Yang, Jan Kautz, Stan Birchfield},
41: title         = {{FoundationPose}: Unified 6D Pose Estimation and Tracking of Novel Objects},
42: booktitle     = {CVPR},
43: year          = {2024},
44: }
45: ```
46: 
47: If you find the model-free setup useful, please also consider cite:
48: 
49: ```bibtex
50: @InProceedings{bundlesdfwen2023,
51: author        = {Bowen Wen and Jonathan Tremblay and Valts Blukis and Stephen Tyree and Thomas M\"{u}ller and Alex Evans and Dieter Fox and Jan Kautz and Stan Birchfield},
52: title         = {{BundleSDF}: {N}eural 6-{DoF} Tracking and {3D} Reconstruction of Unknown Objects},
53: booktitle     = {CVPR},
54: year          = {2023},
55: }
56: ```
57: 
58: # Data prepare
59: 
60: 
61: 1) Download all network weights from [here](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1DFezOAD0oD1BblsXVxqDsl8fj0qzB82i?usp=sharing) and put them under the folder `weights/`. For the refiner, you will need `2023-10-28-18-33-37`. For scorer, you will need `2024-01-11-20-02-45`.
62: 
63: 1) [Download demo data](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1pRyFmxYXmAnpku7nGRioZaKrVJtIsroP?usp=sharing) and extract them under the folder `demo_data/`
64: 
65: 1) [Optional] Download our large-scale training data: ["FoundationPose Dataset"](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1s4pB6p4ApfWMiMjmTXOFco8dHbNXikp-?usp=sharing)
66: 
67: 1) [Optional] Download our preprocessed reference views [here](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1PXXCOJqHXwQTbwPwPbGDN9_vLVe0XpFS?usp=sharing) in order to run model-free few-shot version.
68: 
69: # Env setup option 1: docker (recommended)
70:   ```
71:   cd docker/
72:   docker pull wenbowen123/foundationpose && docker tag wenbowen123/foundationpose foundationpose  # Or to build from scratch: docker build --network host -t foundationpose .
73:   bash docker/run_container.sh
74:   ```
75: 
76: 
77: If it's the first time you launch the container, you need to build extensions. Run this command *inside* the Docker container.
78: ```
79: bash build_all.sh
80: ```
81: 
82: Later you can execute into the container without re-build.
83: ```
84: docker exec -it foundationpose bash
85: ```
86: 
87: For more recent GPU such as 4090, refer to [this](https://github.com/NVlabs/FoundationPose/issues/27).
88: In short, do the following:
89: ```
90: docker pull shingarey/foundationpose_custom_cuda121:latest
91: ```
92: Then modify the bash script to use this image instead of `foundationpose:latest`.
93: 
94: 
95: # Env setup option 2: conda (local)
96: 
97: 1) **Create the environment** (C++ build deps + Python; all on `conda-forge`):
98: 
99: ```bash
100: conda env create -f environment.yml
101: conda activate foundationpose
102: ```
103: 
104: 2) **Install PyTorch** with a CUDA build that matches your machine. The [PyTorch “Get Started”](https://pytorch.org/get-started/locally/) page lists the right `--index-url` (for example `cu124` works on most current NVIDIA drivers). Example:
105: 
106: ```bash
107: python -m pip install torch torchvision torchaudio --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu124
108: ```
109: 
110: 3) **Install PyTorch3D and NVDiffRast** (compile from source; needs the CUDA toolkit for `nvcc`. Point `CUDA_HOME` at your install, e.g. `/usr/local/cuda-12.8` or `/usr/local/cuda`):
111: 
112: ```bash
113: export CUDA_HOME=/usr/local/cuda   # or e.g. /usr/local/cuda-12.8
114: export PATH="$CUDA_HOME/bin:$PATH"
115: python -m pip install --no-build-isolation "git+https://github.com/facebookresearch/pytorch3d.git"
116: python -m pip install --no-build-isolation "git+https://github.com/NVlabs/nvdiffrast.git"
117: ```
118: 
119: `--no-build-isolation` is required so the build can see the `torch` you already installed.
120: 
121: 4) **Install remaining Python dependencies** and **build the `mycpp` extension** (BundleSDF’s `mycuda` step is optional; skip it unless you need the model-free / NeRF path):
122: 
123: ```bash
124: python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
125: bash build_all_conda.sh
126: ```
127: 
128: 5) **Optional — Kaolin** (only for the model-free setup; version must match your PyTorch/CUDA—see the [Kaolin install docs](https://kaolin.readthedocs.io/en/latest/notes/installation.html)).
129: 
130: 
131: # Run model-based demo
132: The paths have been set in argparse by default. If you need to change the scene, you can pass the args accordingly. By running on the demo data, you should be able to see the robot manipulating the mustard bottle. Pose estimation is conducted on the first frame, then it automatically switches to tracking mode for the rest of the video. The resulting visualizations will be saved to the `debug_dir` specified in the argparse. (Note the first time running could be slower due to online compilation)
133: ```
134: python run_demo.py
135: ```
136: 
137: 
138: <img src="assets/demo.jpg" width="50%">
139: 
140: 
141: Feel free to try on other objects (**no need to retrain**) such as driller, by changing the paths in argparse.
142: 
143: <img src="assets/demo_driller.jpg" width="50%">
144: 
145: 
146: # Run on public datasets (LINEMOD, YCB-Video)
147: 
148: For this you first need to download LINEMOD dataset and YCB-Video dataset.
149: 
150: To run model-based version on these two datasets respectively, set the paths based on where you download. The results will be saved to `debug` folder
151: ```
152: python run_linemod.py --linemod_dir /mnt/9a72c439-d0a7-45e8-8d20-d7a235d02763/DATASET/LINEMOD --use_reconstructed_mesh 0
153: 
154: python run_ycb_video.py --ycbv_dir /mnt/9a72c439-d0a7-45e8-8d20-d7a235d02763/DATASET/YCB_Video --use_reconstructed_mesh 0
155: ```
156: 
157: To run model-free few-shot version. You first need to train Neural Object Field. `ref_view_dir` is based on where you download in the above "Data prepare" section. Set the `dataset` flag to your interested dataset.
158: ```
159: python bundlesdf/run_nerf.py --ref_view_dir /mnt/9a72c439-d0a7-45e8-8d20-d7a235d02763/DATASET/YCB_Video/bowen_addon/ref_views_16 --dataset ycbv
160: ```
161: 
162: Then run the similar command as the model-based version with some small modifications. Here we are using YCB-Video as example:
163: ```
164: python run_ycb_video.py --ycbv_dir /mnt/9a72c439-d0a7-45e8-8d20-d7a235d02763/DATASET/YCB_Video --use_reconstructed_mesh 1 --ref_view_dir /mnt/9a72c439-d0a7-45e8-8d20-d7a235d02763/DATASET/YCB_Video/bowen_addon/ref_views_16
165: ```
166: 
167: # Troubleshooting
168: 
169: 
170: - For more recent GPU such as 4090, refer to [this](https://github.com/NVlabs/FoundationPose/issues/27).
171: 
172: - For setting up on Windows, refer to [this](https://github.com/NVlabs/FoundationPose/issues/148).
173: 
174: - If you are getting unreasonable results, check [this](https://github.com/NVlabs/FoundationPose/issues/44#issuecomment-2048141043) and [this](https://github.com/030422Lee/FoundationPose_manual)
175: 
176: # Training data download
177: Our training data include scenes using 3D assets from GSO and Objaverse, rendered with high quality photo-realism and large domain randomization. Each data point includes **RGB, depth, object pose, camera pose, instance segmentation, 2D bounding box**. [[Google Drive]](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1s4pB6p4ApfWMiMjmTXOFco8dHbNXikp-?usp=sharing).
178: 
179: <img src="assets/train_data_vis.png" width="80%">
180: 
181: - To parse the camera params including extrinsics and intrinsics
182:   ```
183:   glcam_in_cvcam = np.array([[1,0,0,0],
184:                           [0,-1,0,0],
185:                           [0,0,-1,0],
186:                           [0,0,0,1]]).astype(float)
187:   W, H = camera_params["renderProductResolution"]
188:   with open(f'{base_dir}/camera_params/camera_params_000000.json','r') as ff:
189:     camera_params = json.load(ff)
190:   world_in_glcam = np.array(camera_params['cameraViewTransform']).reshape(4,4).T
191:   cam_in_world = np.linalg.inv(world_in_glcam)@glcam_in_cvcam
192:   world_in_cam = np.linalg.inv(cam_in_world)
193:   focal_length = camera_params["cameraFocalLength"]
194:   horiz_aperture = camera_params["cameraAperture"][0]
195:   vert_aperture = H / W * horiz_aperture
196:   focal_y = H * focal_length / vert_aperture
197:   focal_x = W * focal_length / horiz_aperture
198:   center_y = H * 0.5
199:   center_x = W * 0.5
200: 
201:   fx, fy, cx, cy = focal_x, focal_y, center_x, center_y
202:   K = np.eye(3)
203:   K[0,0] = fx
204:   K[1,1] = fy
205:   K[0,2] = cx
206:   K[1,2] = cy
207:   ```
208: 
209: 
210: 
211: # Notes
212: Due to the legal restrictions of Stable-Diffusion that is trained on LAION dataset, we are not able to release the diffusion-based texture augmented data, nor the pretrained weights using it. We thus release the version without training on diffusion-augmented data. Slight performance degradation is expected.
213: 
214: # Acknowledgement
215: 
216: We would like to thank Jeff Smith for helping with the code release; NVIDIA Isaac Sim and Omniverse team for the support on synthetic data generation; Tianshi Cao for the valuable discussions. Finally, we are also grateful for the positive feebacks and constructive suggestions brought up by reviewers and AC at CVPR.
217:

## 1. OVERALL DIRECTORY STRUCTURE

```

|-- .git/                           # Git repository metadata

|   |-- demo_robot.gif
|   |-- pipeline.svg
|   |-- tutorial.mp4
|-- conversations/                  # Agent session transcripts (6 markdown files)
|-- demo_data/                      # Object 3D mesh files for pose estimation
|   |-- cube/

|   |-- drill/

|-- FoundationPose/                 # Git submodule clone of NVlabs/FoundationPose
|   |-- [extensive sub-tree; see Section 2]
|-- build_all_conda.sh              # Build script for conda environment
|-- cam_2_base_transform.py         # Camera-to-robot-base coordinate transform
|-- Dockerfile                      # Container definition
|-- foundationpose_ros_multi.py     # MAIN ROS2 entry point script
|-- LICENSE                         # MIT License
|-- README.md                       # Project documentation
|-- requirements.txt                # Python dependencies
|-- run_foundationpose.sh           # Convenience runner script
|-- session-ses_1c65.md             # Agent session log
|-- session-ses_1c656.md            # Agent session log
```

**Key finding:** This is **NOT** a standard ROS2 colcon workspace. There is **no** `package.xml`, **no** `setup.py`/`setup.cfg` at the top level, and **no** `CMakeLists.txt` for ROS2 packaging. The ROS2 node is implemented purely as a standalone Python script (`foundationpose_ros_multi.py`) that depends on rclpy and runs directly with `python` -- it does not require colcon build.

---

## 2. FoundationPose/ DIRECTORY (NVlabs/FoundationPose git clone)

```
FoundationPose/
|-- .git/                           # Its own git repo (clone of NVlabs/FoundationPose)
|-- .gitignore
|-- assets/                         # Images for their readme
|-- bundlesdf/                      # Neural implicit representation (NeRF path)
|   |-- config_linemod.yml
|   |-- config_ycbv.yml
|   |-- mycuda/                     # CUDA ops (has setup.py with C++ flags)
|   |   |-- setup.py
|   |   |-- gridencoder.cpython-310-x86_64-linux-gnu.so (prebuilt)
|   |   |-- build/ (ninja build artifacts)
|   |-- nerf_helpers.py
|   |-- nerf_runner.py
|   |-- run_nerf.py
|   |-- tool.py
|-- check_env.py                    # Environment validation script
|-- datareader.py                   # BOP dataset readers
|-- docker/                         # Docker build files
|   |-- dockerfile
|   |-- run_container.sh
|-- eigen-3.4.0/                    # Eigen linear algebra library (vendored)
|-- environment.yml                 # Conda environment spec (Python 3.11)
|-- estimater.py                    # Core FoundationPose class (270 lines)
|-- learning/                       # Neural network models & training
|   |-- datasets/
|   |   |-- h5_dataset.py
|   |   |-- pose_dataset.py

## 3. CONTENTS OF KEY FILES

### requirements.txt (ROS2 top level)
**File:** `/home/jinwz/FoundationPoseROS2/FoundationPoseROS2/requirements.txt` (55 lines)

Key packages: `torchvision==0.16.0`, `torch==2.1.0`, `torchaudio==2.1.0`, `numpy<2`, `opencv_python==4.10.0.82`, `ultralytics==8.3.31`, `trimesh==4.4.7`, `pytorch_lightning==2.4.0`, `warp==1.0.4`, `scipy==1.14.1`, `pyrender==0.1.45`, `open3d==0.18.0`, `cv_bridge`, `kornia==0.5.10`, `PySide6==6.8.0.1`, `dearpygui==1.11.1`, etc.

### README.md
**File:** `/home/jinwz/FoundationPoseROS2/FoundationPoseROS2/README.md` (175 lines)

- **Title:** FoundationPoseROS2: Multi-Object Pose Estimation and Tracking of Novel Objects in ROS2 with RealSense2
- **Author:** ammar-n-abbas
- **Description:** ROS2-integrated system using FoundationPose + SAM2 for multi-object 6D pose estimation/tracking
- **Prerequisites:** Ubuntu 20/22, ROS2 Foxy/Humble, 8GB NVIDIA GPU, CUDA 12.x, Intel RealSense Camera
- **Setup:** Conda env `foundationpose_ros` with Python 3.10, run `build_all_conda.sh`
- **Important note:** Must change C++14 to C++17 in `FoundationPose/bundlesdf/mycuda/setup.py` lines 18-19
- **Running:** Launch realsense2_camera, then `python foundationpose_ros_multi.py`
- **Features:** Object Selection GUI (Tkinter), SAM2 segmentation, multi-object tracking, 6D pose publishing via PoseStamped

### run_foundationpose.sh
**File:** `/home/jinwz/FoundationPoseROS2/FoundationPoseROS2/run_foundationpose.sh` (27 lines)

Wrapper script that:
1. Activates conda env `foundationpose_ros`
2. Sets `CUDA_HOME` and `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`
3. Sources ROS2 Humble (or Foxy as fallback)
4. Runs `foundationpose_ros_multi.py`

### Dockerfile
**File:** `/home/jinwz/FoundationPoseROS2/FoundationPoseROS2/Dockerfile` (32 lines)

- Based on `ros:humble`
- Installs `ros-humble-cv-bridge`, `ros-humble-sensor-msgs`, `ros-humble-geometry-msgs`
- Pip installs: `numpy`, `scipy`, `trimesh`, `ultralytics`, `pyquaternion`, `pillow`, `rospkg`, `rclpy`
- Entrypoint: `source /opt/ros/humble/setup.bash && python3 main.py`

### foundationpose_ros_multi.py
**File:** `/home/jinwz/FoundationPoseROS2/FoundationPoseROS2/foundationpose_ros_multi.py` (394 lines)

This is THE main ROS2 node. Key structure:

- **Monkey-patches** FoundationPose to add `is_register` flag on `__init__` and `register`
- **FileSelectorGUI** (Tkinter): GUI to reorder .obj/.stl mesh files
- **PoseEstimationNode** (rclpy Node):
  - Subscribes to:
    - `/camera/camera/color/image_raw` (RGB)
    - `/camera/camera/aligned_depth_to_color/image_raw` (Depth)
    - `/camera/camera/color/camera_info` (Camera intrinsics)
  - Uses SAM2 (`ultralytics.SAM("sam2.1_b.pt")`) for segmentation
  - Interactive mask selection via OpenCV mouse callbacks
  - Creates one `FoundationPose` instance per object
  - Publishes `PoseStamped` on topics `/Current_OBJ_position_{idx+1}`
  - Transforms poses from camera frame to base frame via `cam_2_base_transform.py`
- Entry point: scans `demo_data/` for .obj/.stl files, opens GUI, then spins

### build_all_conda.sh
**File:** `/home/jinwz/FoundationPoseROS2/FoundationPoseROS2/build_all_conda.sh` (47 lines)

Steps:
1. Install PyTorch 2.1.0+cu121, torchvision, torchaudio
2. Install pytorch3d from source
3. Install `requirements.txt`
4. Clone FoundationPose repo
5. Download pretrained weights via gdown
6. Build pybind11 (v2.10.0)
7. Install Eigen 3.4.0
8. Clone and install nvdiffrast
9. Build mycpp C++ extensions
10. Build bundlesdf/mycuda CUDA extensions

## 6. ROS2 WORKSPACE STRUCTURE ANALYSIS

**This is NOT a colcon workspace.** Evidence:

1. **No** `package.xml` at any level (zero found)
2. **No** `setup.py` or `setup.cfg` at the top level (only inside FoundationPose submodules)
3. **No** standard ROS2 package directory layout (`src/`, `launch/`, `config/`, etc.)
4. The VS Code settings reference `/opt/ros/humble` but only for extra import paths, not workspace build
5. The script uses `rclpy` directly via `python foundationpose_ros_multi.py`
6. All CMakeLists.txt files found are from subcomponents (pybind11, Eigen, mycpp), not ROS2 packages

**How it runs:** The `run_foundationpose.sh` script simply sources ROS2 setup.bash and runs the Python script directly. The ROS2 node is a standalone Python file that imports rclpy.

### Environment files:

- **FoundationPose/** `requirements.txt` -- Another 43 packages (FoundationPose's own deps)

- **build_all_conda.sh** installs PyTorch 2.1.0+cu121, pytorch3d, and all Python deps

### Key installed packages in `foundationpose_ros` env (confirmed running):
| Package | Version |
|---------|---------|

| CUDA in env | 13.0 |
| torchvision | 0.27.0 |
| ultralytics | 8.4.51 |
| trimesh | 4.12.2 |

### Note on NumPy conflict:
There is a known **NumPy version conflict** -- PyTorch 2.1.0 requires `numpy<2`, but the environment was observed to have NumPy 2.2.6. The `requirements.txt` correctly specifies `numpy<2`, but during a build attempt, the download failed due to network issues (BrokenPipeError). The `mycuda` CUDA extension build also fails because pip's build isolation cannot see the conda-installed torch.

### Git repos:
- **Main repo:** `https://github.com/ammar-n-abbas/FoundationPoseROS2.git` (up to date, no submodules)
- **FoundationPose/** is a manual git clone of `https://github.com/NVlabs/FoundationPose.git` (not a git submodule)
- **FoundationPose/nvdiffrast/** is a manual git clone of `https://github.com/NVlabs/nvdiffrast.git`
- **FoundationPose/pybind11/** is a manual git clone of pybind11 (v2.10.0)

### Build status:
The mycpp C++ extensions and bundlesdf/mycuda CUDA extensions need to be compiled. The `setup.py` at `FoundationPose/bundlesdf/mycuda/setup.py` was already patched from C++14 to C++17 (as confirmed by reading the file). However, the actual `pip install -e .` for mycuda failed due to pip build isolation not finding torch in the conda environment.
